Nat.Partrec.Code.pappAckdefMathlib.Computability.Ackermann, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x$ be a natural number. Then $\operatorname{pappAck}$ applied to $x$ is a code defined as $\operatorname{brecOn}$ applied to $x$ and $\mathrm{Nat\_\_Partrec\_\_Code\_\_pappAck\_\_\_f}$.
Formula
$x \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{pappAck} (x):= \operatorname{brecOn} (x , \mathrm{Nat\_\_Partrec\_\_Code\_\_pappAck\_\_\_f})\in \operatorname{Code}$
French
Soit $x$ un nombre naturel. Alors $\operatorname{pappAck}$ appliqué à $x$ est un code défini comme $\operatorname{brecOn}$ appliqué à $x$ et $\mathrm{Nat\_\_Partrec\_\_Code\_\_pappAck\_\_\_f}$.
Nat.Partrec.Code.pappAck.stepdefMathlib.Computability.Ackermann, function or constant
Wording
the step of c (from the name)
English
Let $c$ be a code. Then the step of $c$ is a code defined as the curry of $\operatorname{prec}$ applied to the composition of $c$ and the constant of $1$ and the composition of $c$ and the composition of the right and the right and $0$.
Formula
$c \in \operatorname{Code} \vdash \operatorname{step} (c):= \operatorname{curry} (\operatorname{prec} (\operatorname{comp} (c , \operatorname{const} (1)), \operatorname{comp} (c , \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{right} , \operatorname{right}))), 0)\in \operatorname{Code}$
French
Soit $c$ un code. Alors l'étape de $c$ est un code défini comme la curryfication de $\operatorname{prec}$ appliqué à la composition de $c$ et la constante de $1$ et la composition de $c$ et la composition du droit et le droit et $0$.
ackdefMathlib.Computability.Ackermann, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x$ and $x_{1}$ be natural numbers. Then $\mathrm{ack}$ applied to $x$ and $x_{1}$ is a natural number defined as $\mathrm{ack\_\_\_unary}$ applied to $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $x$ and $x_{1}$.
Formula
$x , x_{1} \in \Natural \vdash \mathrm{ack} (x , x_{1}):= \mathrm{ack\_\_\_unary} (\operatorname{mk} (x , x_{1}))\in \Natural$
French
Soient $x$ et $x_{1}$ des nombres naturels. Alors $\mathrm{ack}$ appliqué à $x$ et $x_{1}$ est un nombre naturel défini comme $\mathrm{ack\_\_\_unary}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $x$ et $x_{1}$.

Mathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.GrowsPolynomially

AkraBazziRecurrence.GrowsPolynomiallydefMathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.GrowsPolynomially, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a function from real numbers to real numbers. Then $\operatorname{GrowsPolynomially}$ holds for $f$, if for all real numbers $b$, if $b$ is an element of the open interval of $0$ and $1$, then there exists a real number $x$, such that $x > 0$ and there exists a real number $x_{1}$ such that $x_{1} > 0$ and $\operatorname{Eventually}$ holds for the function that maps $x_{2}$ to the proposition that for all real numbers $u$, if $u$ is an element of $\SetIccMACRoP {(b \times x_{2})}{x_{2}}$, then $\MembershipmemMACRoT {\SetIccMACRoP {(x \times f (x_{2}))}{(x_{1} \times f (x_{2}))}}{f (u)}$ and $\operatorname{atTop}$.
Formula
$f \in \Real \rightarrow \Real \vdash \operatorname{GrowsPolynomially} (f):\Leftrightarrow \forall b \in \Real . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{Ioo} (0 , 1)}{b}\rightarrow (\exists x \in \Real . x > 0 \wedge (\exists x_{1} \in \Real . x_{1} > 0 \wedge \operatorname{Eventually} (\lambda x_{2} . \forall u \in \Real . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\SetIccMACRoW {(b \times x_{2})}{x_{2}}}{u}\rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\SetIccMACRoW {(x \times f (x_{2}))}{(x_{1} \times f (x_{2}))}}{f (u)}, \operatorname{atTop})))$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des nombres réels à des nombres réels. Alors $\operatorname{GrowsPolynomially}$ tient pour $f$ si pour tous les nombres réels $b$, si $b$ est un élément de l'intervalle ouvert de $0$ et $1$, alors il existe un nombre réel $x$ tel que $x > 0$ et il existe un nombre réel $x_{1}$ tel que $x_{1} > 0$ et $\operatorname{Eventually}$ tienne pour la fonction qui envoie $x_{2}$ à la proposition que pour tous des nombres réels $u$, si $u$ est un élément de $\SetIccMACRoP {(b \times x_{2})}{x_{2}}$, alors $\MembershipmemMACRoT {\SetIccMACRoP {(x \times f (x_{2}))}{(x_{1} \times f (x_{2}))}}{f (u)}$ et $\operatorname{atTop}$.

Mathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.SumTransform

AkraBazziRecurrencestructureMathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.SumTransform, type
Wording
no wording
English
Let $T$ be a function from natural numbers to real numbers. Let $g$ be a function from real numbers to real numbers. Let $a$ and $b$ be functions from elements of $x$ to real numbers. Let $r$ be a function from elements of $x$ and natural numbers to natural numbers. Then the elements of $\mathrm{AkraBazziRecurrence}$ applied to $T$, $g$, $a$, $b$ and $r$ are a basic type.
Formula
$T \in \Natural \rightarrow \Real , g \in \Real \rightarrow \Real , a , b \in x \rightarrow \Real , r \in x \rightarrow \Natural \rightarrow \Natural \vdash \mathrm{AkraBazziRecurrence} (T , g , a , b , r)\ \mathsf{set}$
French
Soit $T$ une fonction des nombres naturels à des nombres réels. Soit $g$ une fonction des nombres réels à des nombres réels. Soient $a$ et $b$ des fonctions des éléments de $x$ à des nombres réels. Soit $r$ une fonction des éléments de $x$ et des nombres naturels à des nombres naturels. Alors les éléments de $\mathrm{AkraBazziRecurrence}$ appliqué à $T$, $g$, $a$, $b$ et $r$ sont un type de base.
AkraBazziRecurrence.asympBounddefMathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.SumTransform, function or constant
Wording
the asymptotic bound of b and n (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $g$ be a function from real numbers to real numbers. Let $a$ and $b$ be functions from elements of $x$ to real numbers. Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the asymptotic bound of $b$ and $n$ is a real number defined as the sum of $n ^ {\operatorname{p} (a , b)}$ and the sum transform of $0$ and $n$.
Formula
$g \in \Real \rightarrow \Real , a , b \in x \rightarrow \Real , n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{asympBound} (g , a , b , n):= n ^ {\operatorname{p} (a , b)}+ \operatorname{sumTransform} (\operatorname{p} (a , b), g , 0 , n)\in \Real$
French
Soit $g$ une fonction des nombres réels à des nombres réels. Soient $a$ et $b$ des fonctions des éléments de $x$ à des nombres réels. Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Alors la borne asymptotique de $b$ et $n$ est un nombre réel défini comme la somme de $n ^ {\operatorname{p} (a , b)}$ et de la transformée de sommes de $0$ et $n$.

AkraBazziRecurrence.sumTransformdefMathlib.Computability.AkraBazzi.SumTransform, function or constant
Wording
the sum transform of x and n (from the name)
English
Let $p$ be a real number. Let $g$ be a function from real numbers to real numbers. Let $x$ and $n$ be natural numbers. Then the sum transform of $x$ and $n$ is a real number defined as $n ^ {p}\times \FinsetSum {(\FinsetIcoMACRoO {x}{n})}{u}{(\frac{ g (u)}{u ^ {(p + 1)}})}$.
Formula
$p \in \Real , g \in \Real \rightarrow \Real , x , n \in \Natural \vdash \operatorname{sumTransform} (p , g , x , n):= n ^ {p}\times \FinsetSum {(\FinsetIcoMACRoV {x}{n})}{u}{(\frac{ g (u)}{u ^ {(p + 1)}})}\in \Real$
French
Soit $p$ un nombre réel. Soit $g$ une fonction des nombres réels à des nombres réels. Soient $x$ et $n$ des nombres naturels. Alors la transformée de sommes de $x$ et $n$ est un nombre réel défini comme $n ^ {p}\times \FinsetSum {(\FinsetIcoMACRoO {x}{n})}{u}{(\frac{ g (u)}{u ^ {(p + 1)}})}$.

Mathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar

ContextFreeGrammarstructureMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, type
Wording
context free grammar of T (from the name)
English
Let $T$ be a set. Then the context free grammars of $T$ are a basic type.
Formula
$T \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T)\ \mathsf{set}$
French
Soit $T$ un ensemble. Alors les grammaires libres de contextes de $T$ sont un type de base.
ContextFreeGrammar.DerivesdefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $g$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Let $x$ and $x_{1}$ be lists of symbols of $T$ and $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $g$. Then $\operatorname{Derives}$ holds for $g$, $x$ and $x_{1}$, if $\operatorname{ReflTransGen}$ holds for $\operatorname{Produces}$ applied to $g$, $x$ and $x_{1}$.
Formula
$g \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T), x , x_{1} \in \mathrm{List} (\mathrm{Symbol} (T , \operatorname{NT} (g)))\vdash \operatorname{Derives} (g , x , x_{1}):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{ReflTransGen} (\operatorname{Produces} (g), x , x_{1})$
French
Soit $g$ une grammaire libre de contextes de $T$. Soient $x$ et $x_{1}$ des listes de symboles de $T$ et $\operatorname{NT}$ appliqué à $g$. Alors $\operatorname{Derives}$ tient pour $g$, $x$ et $x_{1}$ si $\operatorname{ReflTransGen}$ tient pour $\operatorname{Produces}$ appliqué à $g$, $x$ et $x_{1}$.
ContextFreeGrammar.GeneratesdefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $g$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Let $s$ be a list of symbols of $T$ and $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $g$. Then $\operatorname{Generates}$ holds for $g$ and $s$, if $\operatorname{Derives}$ holds for $g$, $\operatorname{cons}$ applied to $\operatorname{nonterminal}$ applied to $\operatorname{initial}$ applied to $g$ and $\operatorname{nil}$ and $s$.
Formula
$g \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T), s \in \mathrm{List} (\mathrm{Symbol} (T , \operatorname{NT} (g)))\vdash \operatorname{Generates} (g , s):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{Derives} (g , \operatorname{cons} (\operatorname{nonterminal} (\operatorname{initial} (g)), \operatorname{nil}), s)$
French
Soit $g$ une grammaire libre de contextes de $T$. Soit $s$ une liste de symboles de $T$ et $\operatorname{NT}$ appliqué à $g$. Alors $\operatorname{Generates}$ tient pour $g$ et $s$ si $\operatorname{Derives}$ tient pour $g$, $\operatorname{cons}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{nonterminal}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{initial}$ appliqué à $g$ et $\operatorname{nil}$ et $s$.
ContextFreeGrammar.NTdefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, type
Wording
no wording
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Then an element of $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$ is an element of the function that maps $\mathrm{self}$ to $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T)\vdash \operatorname{NT} (\mathrm{self}):= \lambda \mathrm{self} . \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self})$
French
Soit $\mathrm{self}$ une grammaire libre de contextes de $T$. Alors un élément de $\operatorname{NT}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{self}$ est un élément de la fonction qui envoie $\mathrm{self}$ à $\operatorname{proj0}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{self}$.
ContextFreeGrammar.ProducesdefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $g$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Let $u$ and $v$ be lists of symbols of $T$ and $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $g$. Then $\operatorname{Produces}$ holds for $g$, $u$ and $v$, if there exists a context free rule $r$ of $T$ and $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $g$, such that $r$ is an element of the rule of $g$ and $\operatorname{Rewrites}$ holds for $r$, $u$ and $v$.
Formula
$g \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T), u , v \in \mathrm{List} (\mathrm{Symbol} (T , \operatorname{NT} (g)))\vdash \operatorname{Produces} (g , u , v):\Leftrightarrow \exists r \in \mathrm{ContextFreeRule} (T , \operatorname{NT} (g)). \MembershipmemMACRoAA {\operatorname{rules} (g)}{r}\wedge \operatorname{Rewrites} (r , u , v)$
French
Soit $g$ une grammaire libre de contextes de $T$. Soient $u$ et $v$ des listes de symboles de $T$ et $\operatorname{NT}$ appliqué à $g$. Alors $\operatorname{Produces}$ tient pour $g$, $u$ et $v$ s'il existe une règle libre de contextes $r$ de $T$ et $\operatorname{NT}$ appliqué à $g$ tel que $r$ soit un élément de la règle de $g$ et $\operatorname{Rewrites}$ tienne pour $r$, $u$ et $v$.

Wording
the language of g (from the name)
English
Let $g$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Then the language of $g$ is a language of $T$ defined as $\operatorname{ofPred}$ applied to the function that maps $w$ to $\operatorname{Generates}$ applied to $g$ and $\operatorname{map}$ applied to $\operatorname{terminal}$ and $w$.
Formula
$g \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T)\vdash \operatorname{language} (g):= \operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda w . \operatorname{Generates} (g , \operatorname{map} (\operatorname{terminal} , w)))\in \mathrm{Language} (T)$

ContextFreeGrammar.rulesdefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, function or constant
Wording
the rule of self (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a context free grammar of $T$. Then the rule of $\mathrm{self}$ is a finite set of the set of context free rules of $T$ and $\operatorname{NT}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$ defined as $\operatorname{proj2}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T)\vdash \operatorname{rules} (\mathrm{self}):= \operatorname{proj2} (\mathrm{self})\in \mathrm{Finset} (\mathrm{ContextFreeRule} (T , \operatorname{NT} (\mathrm{self})))$

ContextFreeRule.Rewrites.belowinductiveMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, predicate
Wording

ContextFreeRule.reversedefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, function or constant
Wording
the reverse of r (from the name)
English
Let $r$ be a context free rule of $T$ and $N$. Then the reverse of $r$ is a context free rule of $T$ and $N$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to the input of $r$ and $\operatorname{reverse}$ applied to the output of $r$.
Formula
$r \in \mathrm{ContextFreeRule} (T , N)\vdash \operatorname{reverse} (r):= \operatorname{mk} (\operatorname{input} (r), \operatorname{reverse} (\operatorname{output} (r)))\in \mathrm{ContextFreeRule} (T , N)$

Language.IsContextFreedefMathlib.Computability.ContextFreeGrammar, predicate
Wording
L is a context free (from the name)
English
Let $L$ be a language of $T$. Then $L$ is a context free, if there exists a context free grammar $g$ of $T$, such that the language of $g$ is equal to $L$.
Formula
$L \in \mathrm{Language} (T)\vdash \operatorname{IsContextFree} (L):\Leftrightarrow \exists g \in \mathrm{ContextFreeGrammar} (T). \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{language} (g)}{L}$
French

ManyOneDegree.liftOndefMathlib.Computability.Reduce, function or constant

English
Let $d$ be a many one degree. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $\wp \Natural$ to elements of $x$ such that for all elements $p$ and $q$ of $\wp \Natural$, if $\mathrm{ManyOneEquiv}$ holds for the function that maps $x_{1}$ to $\MembershipmemMACRoT {p}{x_{1}}$ and the function that maps $x_{2}$ to $\MembershipmemMACRoT {q}{x_{2}}$, then $\EqtMACRoH {f (p)}{f (q)}$.
